uses a binary operator, then it is equivalent to either
p,\bar{p},\mathsf{True},\mathsf{False}
. The only possibility is that
\varphi
is equivalent to
p
, in which case
\varphi\implies\exists\lF p
. Otherwise, if
\varphi
does not use a negation, then necessarily it uses the proposition
p
. Therefore,
\varphi\in\{p,\exists\lF p,\forall\lF p,\exists\lG p,\forall\lG p,\exists\lX p,\forall\lX p\}
. One can then check that, in all these cases, we have
\varphi\implies\exists\lF p
. Therefore, since
K\models\varphi
, we also have
K\models\exists\lF p
. Hence,
(p,\bar{p},K)
is a positive instance of 1-
\mathsf{Reach}
.

The case
\mathsf{H}=\lG
is dual: in that case, we consider the formula
\varphi:=\forall\lG\bar{p}
.
∎

With the two above lemmas, Theorem
79
follows.

Proof.

The fact that the decision problem
\mathsf{CTL}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
is in
\mathsf{NL}
comes from Lemma
90
. The fact that, if
\lF\in\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}
or
\lG\in\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}
, then it is also
\mathsf{NL}
-hard comes from Lemma
94
and the fact that the reduction given in Definition
93
can be computed in logarithmic space.
∎

4.4
\mathsf{ATL}
learning without the operator
\lX

We have seen in the previous section that
\mathsf{CTL}
learning with the operator
\lX
is
\mathsf{NP}
-complete, while it can be solved in non-deterministic logarithmic space if this operator is not allowed anymore. In this section, we study the learning problem for
\mathsf{ATL}
-formulas, that do not use the operator
\lX
, and concurrent game structures, with two or three agents.

The cases of
\mathsf{ATL}
learning with two or three agents are different. However, we start by giving some central definitions and establishing central lemmas that will be used in both cases for the
\mathsf{NP}
-hardness proof.

4.4.1
Alternating
\mathsf{ATL}
-formulas and turn-based structures

We have introduced the notion of turn-based structures in Section
4.2.2
. We will also use them in this subsection. As exemplified in Figure
6
, whenever we draw turn-based game structures, we will use the following conventions:

•

Blue diamond-shaped states are Agent-1 states;

•

Red rectangle-shaped states are Agent-2 states;

•

Violet octagon-shaped states are Agent-3 states.

a

a

Figure 6:
A Agent-1 state on the left, an Agent-2 state in the middle, and an Agent-3 state on the right.

Let us now introduce several useful definition on turn-based game structures. First of all, we consider self-looping turn-based structures.

Definition 95
.

Let
\alpha\in\{2,3\}
,
\mathsf{Ag}:=[1\,\ldots,\alpha]
and consider a
(0,\emptyset)
-proper
\mathsf{Ag}
-turn-based structure
T
. This structure is
self-looping
if, for all states
q
in
T
, we have that
q\in\mathsf{Succ}(q)
.

Furthermore, we will often use the states
q^{\mathsf{win}}
and
q^{\mathsf{lose}}
which will always satisfy the following:
\mathsf{Succ}(q^{\mathsf{win}}):=\{q^{\mathsf{win}}\}
,
\mathsf{Succ}(q^{\mathsf{lose}}):=\{q^{\mathsf{lose}}\}
,
\pi(q^{\mathsf{win}}):=\{p\}
, and
\pi(q^{\mathsf{lose}}):=\{\bar{p}\}
.

Such structures satisfy the lemma below.

Lemma 96
.

Let
k\in\{2,3\}
and
\mathsf{Ag}:=[1\,\ldots,k]
. For all
\mathsf{ATL}^{k}
-formulas
\phi
, the three
\mathsf{ATL}
-formulas
\phi
,
\langle\langle\emptyset\rangle\rangle\lF\phi
, and
\langle\langle\mathsf{Ag}\rangle\rangle\lG\phi
are equivalent on all
\mathsf{Ag}
-turn-based self-looping structures.

Proof.

By definition of the operators
\lF
and
\lG
, we have
\langle\langle\mathsf{Ag}\rangle\rangle\lG\phi\implies\phi\implies\langle\langle\emptyset\rangle\rangle\lF\phi
. Furthermore, in any
\mathsf{Ag}
-turn-based structure, for any state
q
satisfying
q\in\mathsf{Succ}(q)
, there is a strategy
s
for the coalition of agents
\mathsf{Ag}
such that
\mathsf{Out}^{Q}(q,s)=\{q^{\omega}\}
. It follows that
q\models\emptyset\lF\phi
implies
q\models\phi
, which itself implies
q\models 1,2\lG\phi
.
∎

Let us now introduce below another notion on turn-based structures: (alternating) winning path. In a subsequent definition, we will also introduce the notion of alternating formulas and relate them with alternating winning paths.

Definition 97
.

Let
\alpha\in\{2,3\}
,
\mathsf{Ag}:=[1\,\ldots,\alpha]
and consider a
(0,\emptyset)
-proper self-looping
\mathsf{Ag}
-turn-based structure
T
. Consider a state
q\in Q
. A
winning path
in
T
from
q
is a sequence of states
\rho\in Q^{n+1}
, for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
, such that:
\rho[1]=q
,
\pi(\rho[n+1])=\{p\}
, and for all
1\leq i\leq n
, we have
\pi(\rho[n+1])=\{\bar{p}\}
and
\rho_{i+1}\in\mathsf{Succ}(\rho_{i})
. We let
\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q)
denote the set of all winning paths from
q
.

The winning path
\rho\in\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q)
is
safe
if: for all
1\leq i\leq n
, we have
\mathsf{Succ}(\rho[i])=\{\rho[i],\rho[i+1]\}
and
\mathsf{Succ}(\rho[n+1])=\{\rho[n+1]\}
. Note that, in that case, we have
|\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q)|=1
.

For any two coalitions
A,A^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{Ag}
such that
A\cap A^{\prime}=\emptyset
, we say that the winning path
\rho\in\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q)
is
(A,A^{\prime},n)
-alternating
if: for all
1\leq i\leq n
, we have
\mathsf{AgSt}(\rho[i])\in A\cup A^{\prime}
and for all
1\leq i\leq n-1
, we have
\mathsf{AgSt}(\rho[i])\in A
if and only if
\mathsf{AgSt}(\rho[i+1])\in A^{\prime}
.

Let us now define the notion of alternating
\mathsf{ATL}
-formulas.

Definition 98
.

Let
\alpha\in\{2,3\}
and
\mathsf{Ag}:=[1\,\ldots,\alpha]
. Consider two coalitions
A,A^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{Ag}
such that
A\cap A^{\prime}=\emptyset
(the union is disjoint). For all
\mathsf{O}\subseteq\{\lF,\lG\}
, we let
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\mathsf{O})
denote the set
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\mathsf{O}):=\{\langle\langle B\rangle\rangle\mathsf{H}\mid B\cap A^{\prime}=\emptyset\text{ or }B\cap A=\emptyset,\;\mathsf{H}\in\mathsf{O}\}
. Then,
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\mathsf{O})
-formulas refer to the set of
\mathsf{ATL}(\{p\},\mathsf{O},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
-formulas
\phi
that only use operators in
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\mathsf{O})
.

Then, we say that an
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\mathsf{O})
-formula
\phi
is
(A,A^{\prime},n)
-alternating if (recall Notation
56
):

\phi\in\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\mathsf{O})^{*}\cdot\langle\langle B_{1}\rangle\rangle\lF\cdot\;\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\mathsf{O})^{*}\cdots\;\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\mathsf{O})^{*}\cdot\langle\langle B_{n}\rangle\rangle\lF\cdot\;\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\mathsf{O})^{*}\cdot p

where, for all odd
i\leq n
, we have
A^{\prime}\cap B_{i}\neq\emptyset
, and for all even
i\leq n
, we have
A\cap B_{i}\neq\emptyset
.

We state below a lemma relating alternating formulas and turn-based structures with alternating winning paths.

Lemma 99
.

Let
k\in\{2,3\}
and
\mathsf{Ag}:=[1\,\ldots,k]
. Consider a self-looping structure
T
and let
n\in\mathbb{N}
. We have, for all states
q\in Q
, and two non-empty coalitions
A,A^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{Ag}
such that
A\cap A^{\prime}=\emptyset
:

a)

If all winning paths
\rho\in\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q)
are
(A,A^{\prime},n)
-alternating, then, all
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formulas
\phi
that accept the state
q
are
(A,A^{\prime},n)
-alternating formulas.

b)

If
|A|=|A^{\prime}|=1
and there is a safe winning path from
q
that is
(A,A^{\prime},n)
-alternating, then any
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formula that is
(A,A^{\prime},n)
-alternating accepts the state
q
.

Proof.

Let us first argue the following: if an
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formula
\phi
accepts a state reachable from some state
q^{\prime}\in Q
, then
\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
. Indeed, if
\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q^{\prime})=\emptyset
, then all states
q^{\prime\prime}
reachable from
q^{\prime}
are such that
\pi(q^{\prime\prime})=\{\bar{p}\}
. Therefore, the
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formula
\phi
— that does not use negations and that is such that
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=p
— does not accept any state reachable from
q^{\prime}
.

Now, we prove both items of the lemma by induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Item a) holds when
n=0
since all
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formulas
\phi
are
(A,A^{\prime},0)
-alternating. Furthermore, if there is a safe winning path from
q
that is
(A,A^{\prime},n)
-alternating, it means that
\pi(q)=\{p\}
and
\mathsf{Succ}(q)=\{q\}
. Thus, since any
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formula
\phi
is such that
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=\{p\}
and does not use any negations, Item b) follows.

Assume now that Items a) and b) hold for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Let us first consider Item a). Assume that all winning paths
\rho\in\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q)
are
(A,A^{\prime},n+1)
-alternating. Let us assume that
\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q)\neq\emptyset
, otherwise no
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formula accepts
q
. In particular, it must be that
\mathsf{AgSt}(q)\in A
and
\pi(q)=\{\bar{p}\}
. Let us show by induction on
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formulas
\phi
the following property
\mathcal{P}(\phi)
: if
q\models\phi
, then there is a state
q^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Succ}(q)\setminus\{q\}
and some
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi^{\prime}
such that
q^{\prime}\models\phi^{\prime}
and
\phi\in\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})^{*}\cdot\langle\langle B\rangle\rangle\lF\cdot\phi^{\prime}
such that
A\cap B\neq\emptyset
. The property
\mathcal{P}(p)
holds since
q\not\models p
. Assume now that
\mathcal{P}(\phi)
holds for some
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formula
\phi
. Let
\psi:=\mathsf{O}\cdot\phi
for some
\mathsf{O}\in\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
. Assume that
q\models\psi
. Then, there are two cases:

•

Assume that
\mathsf{O}=\langle\langle B\rangle\rangle\lF
for some coalition
B\subseteq\mathsf{Ag}
. If
q\models\phi
, we can deduce
\mathcal{P}(\psi)
from
\mathcal{P}(\phi)
. Assume now that it is not the case, i.e.
q\not\models\phi
. If
\mathsf{AgSt}(q)\notin B
, for all strategies
s_{B}
of the coalition
B
, we have
q^{\omega}\in\mathsf{Out}^{Q}(q,s_{B})
. This is not possible since
q\models\psi
and
q\not\models\phi
. In fact,
\mathsf{AgSt}(q)\in B
. Since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q)\in A
, this implies
A\cap B\neq\emptyset
. Now, since
q\models\psi
, there is a strategy
s_{B}
for the coalition
B
such that, for all
\rho\in\mathsf{Out}^{Q}(q,s_{B})
, we have
\rho\models\lF\phi
. Consider some
\rho\in\mathsf{Out}^{Q}(q,s_{B})
and let
i\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
be the least index such that
\rho[i]\models\phi
. We have
\rho[i]\in\mathsf{Succ}(q)\setminus\{q\}
. Since there is some
j\geq i
such that
\rho[j]\models\phi
(and
\rho[j]
is reachable from
\rho[i]
), then we have
\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(\rho[i])\neq\emptyset
.
Therefore, since all winning paths from
q
are
(A,A^{\prime},n+1)
-alternating, it follows that
\mathsf{AgSt}(\rho[i])\in A^{\prime}
. Since
A\cap B\neq\emptyset
, we have
A^{\prime}\cap B=\emptyset
(since
\langle\langle B\rangle\rangle\lF\in\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
), and thus
\mathsf{AgSt}(\rho[i])\notin B
. Furthermore, since
\rho\in\mathsf{Out}^{Q}(q,s_{B})
, it follows that the strategy
s_{B}
chooses to go to
\rho[i]
after looping
i-1
times on
q
. Thus, since
\rho[i]\in\mathsf{Succ}(\rho[i])
, we have
q^{i-1}\cdot(\rho[i])^{\omega}\in\mathsf{Out}^{Q}(q,s_{B})
and
q^{i-1}\cdot(\rho[i])^{\omega}\models\lF\phi
. Hence, since
q\not\models\phi
, it follows that
\rho[i]\models\phi
. That is, the property
\mathcal{P}(\psi)
holds.

•

Assume that
\mathsf{O}=\langle\langle B\rangle\rangle\lG
for some coalition
B\subseteq\mathsf{Ag}
. Then,
\psi\implies\phi
, thus
q\models\phi
and we can the deduce
\mathcal{P}(\psi)
from
\mathcal{P}(\phi)
.

We deduce that
\mathcal{P}(\phi)
holds for all
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formulas
\phi
. Therefore, for all
\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formulas
\phi
such that
q\models\phi
, we have that there is a state
q^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Succ}(q)\setminus\{q\}
and some
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi^{\prime}
such that
q^{\prime}\models\phi^{\prime}
and
\phi\in\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})^{*}\cdot\langle\langle B\rangle\rangle\lF\cdot\phi^{\prime}
such that
A\cap B\neq\emptyset
. Since all the winning paths from
q
are
(A,A^{\prime},n)
-alternating, it follows that all the winning paths from
q^{\prime}
are
(A^{\prime},A,n)
-alternating. Thus, by our induction hypothesis, we have that
\phi^{\prime}
is an
(A^{\prime},A,n)
-alternating formula, and therefore
\phi
is an
(A,A^{\prime},n+1)
-alternating formula. Hence, Item a) also holds at index
n+1
.

Consider now Item b). Assume that
|A|=|A^{\prime}|=1
and that there is a safe winning path from
q
that is
(A,A^{\prime},n+1)
-alternating. We let
\rho\in\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q)
. Note that
\rho[1]=q
with
\mathsf{AgSt}(q)\in A
. Consider any
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi
that is
(A^{\prime},A,n)
-alternating. Let
\psi:=\langle\langle B\rangle\rangle\lF\cdot\phi
for some coalition
B
such that
A\cap B\neq\emptyset
. Since
|A|=1
, this implies
\mathsf{AgSt}(q)\in B
. Let us show that
q\models\psi
. The coalition of agents
B
has a strategy
s_{B}
to ensure that, for all
\rho^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Out}^{Q}(q,s_{B})
, we have
\rho^{\prime}[2]=\rho[2]
. Therefore, since the winning path
\rho[2:]
is safe and is
(A^{\prime},A,n)
-alternating, it follows by our induction hypothesis that
\rho[2]\models\phi
. Hence,
q=\rho[1]\models\psi
. Furthermore, for all
2\leq i\leq n+2
, there is a safe winning path from
\rho[i]
that is
(A,A^{\prime},n+2-i)
-alternating or
(A^{\prime},A,n+2-i)
-alternating. Hence, since
\psi
is both
(A,A^{\prime},n+2-i)
-alternating and
(A^{\prime},A,n+2-i)
-alternating, by our induction hypothesis, we have
\rho[i]\models\psi
. Furthermore, since the winning path
\rho\in\mathsf{WinPath}_{T}(q)
is safe, it follows that the set of states reachable from
q
in
T
is equal to
\{\rho[i]\mid 1\leq i\leq n+2\}
. Hence, from all states
q^{\prime}
reachable from
q
, we have
q^{\prime}\models\psi
. We can then deduce that, for all
\mathsf{O}\in\mathsf{Op}_{A,A^{\prime}}(\{\lF,\lG\})^{*}
, we have
q\models\mathsf{O}\cdot\psi
. Therefore, Item b) also holds at index
n+1
. The lemma follows.
∎

We conclude this section with a definition of alternating turn-based structures that we will use in the both proof of
\mathsf{NP}
-hardness for
\mathsf{ATL}
learning. We give the formal definition below, it is illustrated on Figures
7
and
8
.

Definition 100
.

Let
i\neq j\in\{1,2,3\}
. We let:

•

For all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
Q^{l:\;i,j}:=\{q_{h}^{i,j}\mid 1\leq h\leq l\}\cup\{q^{\mathsf{win}}\}
;

•

For all
h\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{i,j}_{2h-1}):=j
and
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{i,j}_{2h}):=i
;

•

For all
h\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Succ}(q_{h}^{i,j}):=\begin{cases}\{q^{i,j}_{h},q^{i,j}_{h-1}\}&\text{ if }h>1\\
\{q^{i,j}_{1},q^{\mathsf{win}}\}&\text{ if }h=1\\
\end{cases}

Then, for all
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
, we define the turn-based structure
T^{l:\;i,j}=\langle Q^{l:\;i,j},I_{i,j,l},2,\{p\},\pi,\mathsf{AgSt},\mathsf{Succ}\rangle
where
I_{i,j,l}:=\{q_{l}^{i,j}\}
.

Figure 7:
The turn based game structure
T^{6:\;1,2}
.

Figure 8:
The turn based game structure
T^{6:\;2,3}
.

4.4.2
\mathsf{ATL}
learning with two agents and operators
\lF
and
\lG

In this section, we focus on the case of
\mathsf{ATL}
learning with two agents (Agent 1 and Agent 2). The goal of this subsection is to show the theorem below.

Theorem 101
.

Consider a set
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Un}}^{\mathsf{}}
of unary temporal operators such that
\{\lF,\lG\}\subseteq\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\subseteq\{\lF,\lG,\neg\}
. Then, for all sets
\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Bin}}^{\mathsf{lg}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
is
\mathsf{NP}
-complete.

Overview of the reduction.

As for the
\mathsf{CTL}
reduction, we follow the steps described in Section
4.2.1
, with Step
a)
already taken care of in Section
4.2.2
. Thus, we focus on
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}
-formulas using only unary operators (and a single proposition). First, we define turn-based structures ensuring that: the proposition used is
p
, and the operators
\langle\langle\{1,2\}\rangle\rangle\lF,\langle\langle\emptyset\rangle\rangle\lG,\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lG
are not used. Since all the structures that we use are self-looping, Lemma
96
gives that the operators
\langle\langle\{1,2\}\rangle\rangle\lG,\langle\langle\emptyset\rangle\rangle\lF
are useless. Hence, we can focus on formulas using only the operators
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF,\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lF,\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lG
and the proposition
p
, which are
\mathsf{Op}_{\{1\},\{2\}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formulas.

Fix an instance
(l,C,k)
of the hitting set problem. We consider the bound
B:=3l+1-k
. Our idea is to focus on
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating formulas. To do so, we consider
T^{2l:\;1,2}
as positive structure and use Lemma
99
(Item a). Note that these
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating formulas feature at least
l
occurrences of the operator
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF
and
l
occurrences of the operator
\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lF
. With the proposition occurring in the formula of size at most
B
, there remains
l-k
operators to use. In fact, we define a negative structure
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lG\geq k+1}
(see Figure
10
) that is accepted by any
\mathsf{Op}_{\{1\},\{2\}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formula featuring at least
k+1
sequences
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF\cdot\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lF
where the operators
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF
and
\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lF
are not separated by an operator
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lG
. That way, we ensure that the remaining
l-k
operators are
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lG
operators separating
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF
and
\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lF
. Hence, the formulas
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(l,H)
that we consider are
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating, features exactly
l-k
operators
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lG
. The exact positions of these operators are given by the subset
H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
. Note that, for this reduction, we use the fact that if there is a hitting set of size at most
k
, then there is one of size exactly
k
.

Then, there remains to define, given a subset
C\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
, a positive turn-based structure
T_{l,C,2}
such that
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(l,H)
accepts
T_{l,C,2}
if and only if
H\cap C\neq\emptyset
. The structure
T_{l,C,2}
(see Figure
11
) is analogous to the structure
T^{2l:\;1,2}
except that the final state reached is
q^{\mathsf{lose}}
instead of
q^{\mathsf{win}}
. However, the Agent-1 states corresponding to the indices
i\in C
may not only continue towards the state
q^{\mathsf{lose}}
, but also branch to a Agent-2 testing state that can branch to the losing state
q^{\mathsf{lose}}
, or that can branch to the actual structure
T^{2(i-1):\;1,2}
. That way, these testing states are rejected by all
\mathsf{Op}_{\{1\},\{2\}}(\{\lF,\lG\})
-formulas starting with the operator
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lG
. Overall, we do obtain the desired equivalence.

Formal definitions and proofs.
For readability, we will use the notations below.

Notation 102
.

For the coalition of agent
A=\{i\}
with
i\in\{1,2\}
, the operators
\langle\langle A\rangle\rangle\lF
and
\langle\langle A\rangle\rangle\lG
will be denoted
i\lF
and
i\lG
respectively.

Now, the first step that we take is to define two simple turn-based game structures that will restrict the set of operators that we need to consider.

Definition 103
.

We define the trivial game structure
T_{p}:=\langle\{q^{\mathsf{win}}\},\{q^{\mathsf{win}}\},2,\{p\},\pi,\mathsf{Ag},\mathsf{Succ}\rangle
.

We also define the two-state turn-based game
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\emptyset\lG,2\lG}:=\langle\{q,q^{\mathsf{lose}}\},\{q\},2,\{p,\bar{p}\},\pi,\mathsf{Ag},\mathsf{Succ}\rangle
where
\mathsf{AgSt}(q):=1
,
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{\mathsf{lose}}):=1
,
\mathsf{Succ}(q):=\{q,q^{\mathsf{lose}}\}
,
\pi(q):=\{p\}
.

Figure 9:
The game
T_{p}
on the left and the game
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\emptyset\lG,2\lG}
on the right.

These two games are depicted in Figure
9
, and they satisfy the property below.

Lemma 104
.

Consider any
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi\in\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
. It accepts both
T_{p}
and
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\emptyset\lG,2\lG}
if and only if
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=\{p\}
and it does not use the operators
\emptyset\lG
or
2\lG
.

Proof.

If the proposition
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=\{\bar{p}\}
, then
\phi\not\models T_{p}
. Hence,
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=\{\bar{p}\}
(note that
|\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)|=1
because
\phi
only uses unary operators).

Let us now show by induction the following property: an
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi\in\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(\{p\},\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
accepts
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\emptyset\lG,2\lG}
if and only if it does not use the operators
\emptyset\lG
or
2\lG
. This holds straightforwardly for
\phi=p
. Assume now that it holds for some formula
\phi\in\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(\{p\},\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
accepts
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\emptyset\lG,2\lG}
. Let
\phi^{\prime}=\langle\langle A\rangle\rangle\textbf{H}\phi
with
A\subseteq\{1,2\}
and
\textbf{H}\in\{\lF,\lG\}
.

•

Assume that either
\emptyset\lG
or
2\lG
occurs in
\phi^{\prime}
. If
\emptyset\lG
or
2\lG
occurs in
\phi
, then by our induction hypothesis, we have
q\not\models\phi
. Since we also have
q^{\mathsf{lose}}\not\models\phi
(since
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=\{p\}
) it follows that
q\not\models\phi^{\prime}
. Otherwise, we have
\phi^{\prime}=\langle\langle A\rangle\rangle\lG\phi
with
1\notin A
. Since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q)=1
, for all strategies
s
for the coalition
A
, we have
q\cdot(q^{\mathsf{lose}})^{\omega}\in\mathsf{Out}^{Q}(q,s)
with
q^{\mathsf{lose}}\not\models\phi
. Therefore,
q\not\models\phi^{\prime}
.

•

Assume that neither
\emptyset\lG
nor
2\lG
occurs in
\phi^{\prime}
. By our induction hypothesis, we have
q\models\phi
. Hence, if
\textbf{H}=\lF
, we have
q\models\phi^{\prime}
. Otherwise,
1\in A
. Since the Agent-1 strategy
s_{1}
that always loops on
q
is such that
\mathsf{Out}^{Q}(q,s)=\{q^{\omega}\}
, it follows that
q\models\phi^{\prime}
.

Thus, the property also holds for
\phi^{\prime}
. In fact it holds for all formulas in
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(\{p\},\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
. The lemma follows.
∎

Furthermore, we have seen in Lemma
96
that the operators
\emptyset\lF
and
1,2\lG
are useless on self-looping structures. Thus, we restrict ourselves to promising formulas, i.e. formulas that only use the operators
1\lF,2\lF
and
1\lG
. Note that we have not handled the operator
1,2\lF
6
6

6

That is, we have not shown that we can restrict ourselves to formulas that do not use this operator.
yet. It will be done on the fly later.

Definition 105
.

An
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
formula is
promising
if
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=\{p\}
and it only uses the operators
1\lF,2\lF
, and
1\lG
.

Let us now consider the exact kinds of formulas that we consider, along with the structures that we define to encode the hitting set problem.

Bounding the size of hitting sets

As mentioned at the beginning of this subsection, the way we encode a hitting set is by considering when the operator
1\lG
is used between the operators
1\lF
and
2\lF
. More precisely, we define the notion of concise formulas, a special kind of alternating formulas.

Definition 106
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
. For all
H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
, we denote by
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(l,H)
the promising
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula defined by:

\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(l,H):=1\lF\;\mathsf{Qt}_{l}\;2\lF\;1\lF\mathsf{Qt}_{l-1}\;2\lF\ldots\;1\lF\mathsf{Qt}_{1}\;2\lF p

where, for all
i\in[1,\ldots,l]
, we have
\mathsf{Qt}_{k}\in\{\epsilon,1\lG\}
and
\mathsf{Qt}_{i}=\epsilon
if and only if
i\in H
.

For all
0\leq k\leq l
, we say that a promising
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi
is
(l,k)
-
concise
if it is equal to
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(l,H)
for some
H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
with
|H|=k
. (In which case,
|\phi|=3l+1-k
.)

Let us now define the turn-based structure that will force the use of a minimal number of
1\lG
operators, an example of which is depicted in Figure
10
. This corresponds to the fact that, in the reduction, hitting sets have a bounded size.

Definition 107
.

Let
i\in\{1,2\}
. For all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
, we let
T_{\mathsf{no}\;1\lG\geq k}:=\langle Q^{1\lG}_{k},I_{k},2,\{p\},\pi,\mathsf{Ag},\mathsf{Succ}\rangle
where:

•

Q^{1\lG}_{k}:=\{q_{h}^{1\lG}\mid 1\leq h\leq 2k\}\cup\{q^{\mathsf{win}},q^{\mathsf{lose}}\}
;

•

I_{k}:=\{q_{2k}^{1\lG}\}
;

•

For all
1\leq h\leq k
,
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{1\lG}_{2h-1}):=2
and
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{1\lG}_{2h}):=1
;

•

For all
1\leq h\leq k
, we have:

\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2h}^{1\lG}):=\{q^{1\lG}_{2h},q^{1\lG}_{2h-1}\}

\displaystyle\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2h-1}^{1\lG}):=\begin{cases}\{q_{2h-1}^{1\lG},q_{2h-2}^{1\lG},q^{\mathsf{lose}}\}&\text{ if }h>1\\
\{q_{2h-1}^{1\lG},q^{\mathsf{win}},q^{\mathsf{lose}}\}&\text{ if }h=1\\
\end{cases}

•

For all
q\in Q^{1\lG}_{k}\setminus\{q^{\mathsf{win}}\}
,
\pi(q):=\{\bar{p}\}
.

Figure 10:
The turn-based game structure
T_{\mathsf{no}\;1\lG\geq 4}
.

Let us consider the lemma linking concise formulas and the turn-based structures
T_{\mathsf{no}\;1\lG\geq k}
.

Lemma 108
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
. Consider a promising
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi
that is
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating. Let
k\leq l
. If
|\phi|\leq 3l+1-k
, we have the following equivalence:

q^{1\lG}_{2(k+1)}\not\models\phi
if and only if
\phi
is
(l,k)
-concise

Proof.

We prove by induction on
n\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
the property
\mathcal{P}(n)
stating the lemma for pairs
(l,k)
such that
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
0\leq k\leq l
, and
l+k\leq n
. We start with the case
n=0
. The only possible pair is
l=1
, and
k=0
. Consider a promising
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi
that is
(\{1\},\{2\},2)
-alternating and such that
|\phi|\leq 4
. We have
\phi=\mathsf{Qt}_{1}\;1\lF\;\mathsf{Qt}_{2}\;2\lF\;\mathsf{Qt}_{3}\;p
, with
\mathsf{Qt}_{1},\mathsf{Qt}_{2},\mathsf{Qt}_{3}\in\{\epsilon,1\lF,1\lG,2\lF\}
and at most one of them not equal to
\epsilon
. One can then check that
q_{2}^{\lG}\not\models\phi
if and only
\mathsf{Qt}_{2}=1\lG
, and thus
\mathcal{P}(1)
follows.

Consider now some
n\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
and assume that
\mathcal{P}(n)
holds. Let us show that
\mathcal{P}(n+1)
holds. Consider any pair
(l,k)
such that
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
,
0\leq k\leq l
, and
l+k=n+1
. Consider a promising
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi
that is
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating and such that
|\phi|\leq 3l+1-k
. We let:

\phi=\mathsf{Qt}_{1}\;1\lF\;\mathsf{Qt}_{2}\;2\lF\;\phi^{\prime}

for some
\mathsf{Qt}_{1}\in(1\lG,2\lF)^{*}
,
\mathsf{Qt}_{2}\in(1\lF,1\lG)^{*}
, and sub-formula
\phi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{SubF}(\phi)
. By definition,
\phi^{\prime}
is
(\{1\},\{2\},2(l-1))
-alternating. First of all, note that, since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2(k+2)}^{\lG})=1
, letting
\psi:=1\lF\;\mathsf{Qt}_{2}\;2\lF\;\phi^{\prime}
, we have that
q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG}\models\phi
if and only if
q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG}\models\psi
. There are two cases.

•

Assume that
\mathsf{Qt}_{2}\in(1\lF)^{*}
. If
q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG}\not\models\psi
, then we have
k\geq 1
(since
q^{\mathsf{win}}\models\phi^{\prime}
) and it must be that
q_{2k}^{\lG}\not\models\phi^{\prime}
. Furthermore,
\phi^{\prime}
is
(\{1\},\{2\},2(l-1))
-alternating, and
|\phi^{\prime}|\leq|\phi|-2\leq 3l+1-k=3(l-1)+1-(k-1)
. Therefore, by
\mathcal{P}(n)
(applied to the pair
(l-1,k-1)
), we have that
\phi^{\prime}
is
(l-1,k-1)
-concise, and of size
|\phi^{\prime}|=3(l-1)+1-(k-1)
. Since
|\phi|\leq 3l+1-k
, it follows that
\mathsf{Qt}_{1}=\mathsf{Qt}_{2}=\epsilon
, and
\phi=1\lF 2\lF\phi^{\prime}
is
(l,k)
-concise.

On the other hand, if
\psi
is
(l,k)
-concise, since
\mathsf{Qt}_{2}\neq 1\lG
, it follows that
k\geq 1
,
\phi=1\lF 2\lF\phi^{\prime}
with
\phi^{\prime}
a formula that is
(l-1,k-1)
-concise. Hence, by
\mathcal{P}(n)
(applied to the pair
(l-1,k-1)
), we have
q_{2k}^{\lG}\not\models\phi^{\prime}
. This implies that all sub-formulas
\phi^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{SubF}(\phi^{\prime})
are also such that
q_{2k}^{\lG}\not\models\phi^{\prime\prime}
(because
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2k}^{\lG})=1
, and
\phi^{\prime}\in(1\lF,1\lG,2\lF)^{*}\cdot\phi^{\prime\prime}
). It follows that it is also the case of all the sub-formulas of
\phi
. Therefore, since
\pi(q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG})=\pi(q_{2k+1}^{\lG})=\{\bar{p}\}
, and
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=\{p\}
, we have
q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG}\not\models\phi
.

•

Assume now
\mathsf{Qt}_{2}\notin(1\lF)^{*}
. First of all, this implies
k\leq l-1
. Indeed, if
k=l
, we have
|\phi|\leq 2l+1
, and thus, since it is a
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating formula, it is equal to
\phi=(1\lF 2\lF)^{*}p
, and thus
\mathsf{Qt}_{2}=\epsilon
. In fact, we do have
k\leq l-1
. Furthermore, since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG})=1
and
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2k+1}^{\lG})=2
and
q^{\mathsf{lose}}\not\models\phi^{\prime}
, we have
q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG}\models\psi
if and only if
q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG}\not\models\phi^{\prime}
. By
\mathcal{P}(n)
(applied to the pair
(l-1,k)
), we have
q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG}\not\models\phi^{\prime}
if and only if
\phi^{\prime}
is
(l-1,k)
-concise.
Furthermore, if
\phi^{\prime}
is
(l-1,k)
-concise, we have
|\phi^{\prime}|=3(l-1)+1-k
. Since
|\phi|\leq 3l+1-k
and
\mathsf{Qt}_{2}\notin(1\lF)^{*}
, it follows that
\phi=1\lF 1\lG 2\lF\phi^{\prime}
. In fact,
\phi^{\prime}
is
(l-1,k)
-concise if and only if
\phi
is
(l,k)
-concise. Overall, we obtain that
q_{2(k+1)}^{\lG}\not\models\phi
if and only if
\phi
is
(l,k)
-concise.

Hence, we have established
\mathcal{P}(n+1)
. In fact,
\mathcal{P}(n)
holds for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and the lemma follows.

∎

Hitting sets should intersect the sets
C_{i}

Let us now define the positive turn-based structures that encode the fact that the positions where there is a lack of operators
1\lG
should match the subsets of integers
C_{i}\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
from the hitting set problem. We give the formal definition below, it is illustrated in Figure
11
.

Definition 109
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
and
C\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
. We let
T_{l,C,2}:=\langle Q^{l,C},I_{l},2,\{p\},\pi,\mathsf{Ag},\mathsf{Succ}\rangle
where (recall that the states
q^{1,2}_{h}
come from the turn-based structure
T_{2l:\;1,2}
from Definition
100
):

•

Q^{l,C,2}:=\{q_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq 2l\}\cup\{q^{1,2}_{h}\mid 1\leq h\leq 2l\}\cup\{q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1}\mid i\in C\}\cup\{q^{\mathsf{lose}},q^{\mathsf{win}}\}
;

•

I_{l}:=\{q_{2l}\}
;

•

For all
1\leq i\leq l
,
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2i}):=1
and
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2i-1}):=2
. For all
i\in C
, we have
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1}):=2

•

For all
1\leq i\leq l
, we have:

\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2i}):=\begin{cases}\{q_{2i},q_{2i-1}\}&\text{ if }i\notin C\\
\{q_{2i},q_{2i-1},q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1}\}&\text{ if }i\in C\\
\end{cases}

and

\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2i-1}):=\begin{cases}\{q_{2i-1},q_{2(i-1)}\}&\text{ if }i>1\\
\{q_{2i-1},q^{\mathsf{lose}}\}&\text{ if }i=1\\
\end{cases}

and, for all
i\in C
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{Succ}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1}):=\begin{cases}\{q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1},q^{\mathsf{lose}},q_{2(i-1)}^{1,2}\}&\text{ if }i>1\\
\{q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1},q^{\mathsf{lose}},q^{\mathsf{win}}\}&\text{ if }i=1\\
\end{cases}

•

For all
q\in Q^{l,C,2}\setminus\{q^{\mathsf{win}}\}
,
\pi(q):=\{\bar{p}\}
.

Figure 11:
The turn-based game structure
T_{4,\{2,3\},2}
.

The above definition satisfies the lemma below.

Lemma 110
.

Consider any
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
and
C,H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
. We have:

T_{l,C,2}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(l,H)\text{ if and only if }H\cap C\neq\emptyset

Proof.

For all
1\leq i\leq l
, we let
H_{i}:=H\cap[1,\ldots,l]
. Then, in the turn-based structure
T_{l,C,2}
, we prove by induction on
1\leq i\leq l
the property
\mathcal{P}(i)
:
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
if and only if
H_{i}\cap C\neq\emptyset
. We start with the case
i=1
. There are two cases.

•

Assume that
H_{1}\cap C=\{1\}
. Then, we have
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(1,H_{1})=1\lF 2\lF p
. Furthermore,
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1}\in Q^{l,C,2}
with
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1}\models 2\lF p
since
q^{\mathsf{win}}\in\mathsf{Succ}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1})
. Therefore,
q_{2}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(1,H_{1})
since
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1}\in\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2})
.

•

Assume now that
H_{1}\cap C=\emptyset
. If
1\in C
, we have
1\notin H
, thus
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(1,H_{1})=1\lF 1\lG 2\lF p
. Since
q^{\mathsf{lose}}\in\mathsf{Succ}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1})
, we have
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1}\not\models 1\lG 2\lF p
and
q_{1}\not\models 1\lG 2\lF p
. Hence,
q_{2}\not\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(1,H_{1})
. On the other hand, if
1\notin C
, there is no winning path from
q_{2}
, thus
q_{2}\not\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(1,H_{1})
by Lemma
99
(Item a).

Hence, the property
\mathcal{P}(1)
holds. Assume now that
\mathcal{P}(i)
holds for some
1\leq i\leq l-1
. We have:

\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i+1,H_{i+1})=1\lF\;\mathsf{Qt}_{i+1}\;2\lF\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})

with
\mathsf{Qt}_{i+1}=1\lG
if
i+1\notin H
and
\mathsf{Qt}_{i+1}=\epsilon
otherwise. As above, there are two cases.

•

Assume that
i+1\in H\cap C
. The only winning path from
q_{2i}^{1,2}
is
(\{1\},\{2\},2i)
-alternating and safe. Therefore, by Lemma
99
(Item b), we have
q_{2i}^{1,2}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
. Hence,
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i+1}\in Q^{l,C}
with
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i+1}\models 2\lF\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
. Therefore,
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i+1,H_{(i+1)})
.

•

Assume now that
i+1\notin H\cap C
. Let us show that
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i+1,H_{i+1})
if and only if
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
. First, if
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
, then
q_{2i+1}\models 2\lF\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
and
q_{2i+1}\models 1\lG 2\lF\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
. Thus, we have
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i+1,H_{i+1})
. Assume now that
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i+1,H_{i+1})
. Note that, the winning paths from
q_{2(i+1)}
are all
(\{1\},\{2\},2(i+1))
-alternating, therefore, by Lemma
99
(Item a), no strict sub-formula of
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i+1,H_{i+1})
accept the state
q_{2(i+1)}
. Similarly, the winning paths from
q_{2i+1}
are all
(\{2\},\{1\},2i+1)
-alternating, therefore, by Lemma
99
(Item a), no strict sub-formula of
2\lF\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
accept the state
q_{2i+1}
.
Then, there are two cases.

–

If
i+1\in C
, we have
i+1\notin H
, thus
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i+1,H_{i+1})=1\lF 1\lG 2\lF\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
. Since
q^{\mathsf{lose}}\in\mathsf{Succ}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i+1})
, we have
\mathsf{Succ}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i+1})\not\models 1\lG 2\lF\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
. Hence, with what we have argued above, we have
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
.

–

If
i+1\notin C
, then
\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2(i+1)})=\{q_{2(i+1)},q_{2i+1}\}
, hence as for the previous item, we have that
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
.

We have established that
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i+1,H_{i+1})
if and only if
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
. Furthermore, by
\mathcal{P}(i)
, we have
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
if and only if
H_{i}\cap C\neq\emptyset
. Since
i+1\notin H\cap C
, it follows that
H_{i+1}\cap C=H_{i}\cap C
. Hence, we do obtain that
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i+1,H_{i+1})
if and only if
H_{i+1}\cap C\neq\emptyset
.

Hence,
\mathcal{P}(i+1)
holds. In fact,
\mathcal{P}(i)
holds for all
1\leq i\leq l
. The lemma follows.
∎

Definition of the reduction

We can finally define the reduction that we consider.

Definition 111
.

Consider an instance
(l,C,k)
of the hitting set problem
\mathsf{Hit}
. We define:

•

\mathcal{P}:=\{T_{p},T_{\mathsf{no}\;\emptyset\lG,2\lG}\}\cup\{T_{2l:1,2}\}\cup\{T_{(l,C_{i},2)}\mid 1\leq i\leq n\}
;

•

\mathcal{N}:=\{T_{\mathsf{no}\;1\lG\geq k+1}\}\cup\{T_{2l+1:2,1}\}
.

Then, we define the input
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2),\lF,\lG}_{(l,C,k)}:=(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{N},3l+1-k)
.

This definition satisfies the lemma below.

Lemma 112
.

Let
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Un}}^{\mathsf{}}
be a set of unary temporal operators such that
\{\lF,\lG\}\subseteq\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\subseteq\{\lF,\lG,\neg\}
.
Consider an input
(l,C,k)
of the hitting set problem
\mathsf{Hit}
and the corresponding input
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}_{(l,C,k)}
. Then,
(l,C,k)
is a positive instance of the decision problem
\mathsf{Hit}
if and only if
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of the decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\setminus\{\neg\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
if and only if
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of the decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
.

Proof.

Assume that
(l,C,k)
is a positive instance of the decision problem
\mathsf{Hit}
. Consider a hitting set
H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
of size at most
k
. We let
H\subseteq H^{\prime}\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
be another hitting set of size exactly
k
. We let
\phi:=\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(l,H^{\prime})
. We have
\phi\in\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\setminus\{\neg\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
and
|\phi|=3l+1-k
. Furthermore, since
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=\{p\}
and
\phi
does not use the operators
\emptyset\lG,2\lG
, by Lemma
104
, it accepts both structures
T_{p},T_{\mathsf{no}\;\emptyset\lG,2\lG}\in\mathcal{P}
. In addition,
\phi
is
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating, hence by Lemma
99
(Item b), it accepts
T_{2l:1,2}\in\mathcal{P}
(since there is a safe winning path from
q_{2l}^{1,2}
— in
T_{2l:1,2}
— that is
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating). On the other hand, all winning paths from
q_{2l+1}^{2,1}
are
(\{2\},\{1\},2l+1)
-alternating, while
\phi
is not
(\{2\},\{1\},2l+1)
-alternating, hence by Lemma
99
(Item a),
\phi
rejects the structure
T_{2l+1:2,1}\in\mathcal{N}
. In addition, by Lemma
108
, since
\phi
is
(l,k)
-concise, it rejects
T_{\mathsf{no}\;1\lG\geq k+1}\in\mathcal{N}
. Finally, consider any
1\leq i\leq n
. Since we have
C_{i}\cap H^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
, it follows, by Lemma
110
, that
\phi
accepts
T_{(l,C_{i},2)}\in\mathcal{P}
. Overall, the
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula
\phi
accepts
\mathcal{P}
and rejects
\mathcal{N}
. Hence,
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of the decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\setminus\{\neg\},\emptyset,\emptyset,2)
.

Furthermore, clearly, if
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of the decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\setminus\{\neg\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
, then it is also a positive instance of
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of the decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
.

Assume now that
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of the decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
. Consider a formula
\phi\in\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
with
|\phi|\leq 3l+1-k
that accepts
\mathcal{P}
and rejects
\mathcal{N}
. We let
\phi=\mathsf{Qt}\cdot x
for some
\mathsf{Qt}\in(\mathsf{Op}(2,\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}))^{*}
and
x\in\mathsf{Prop}_{0}
. Let
(\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime},x^{\prime}):=\mathsf{UnNeg}(\mathsf{Qt},0)
. We let
y\in\mathsf{Prop}_{0}
be such that
y=x
if and only if
x^{\prime}=0
. Finally, we let
\phi^{\prime}:=\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}\cdot y
. By Lemma
61
, we have:

•

|\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}|\leq|\mathsf{Qt}|
, therefore
|\phi^{\prime}|\leq|\phi|\leq 3l+1-k
;

•

\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Op}(2,\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\setminus\{\neg\})
, therefore
\phi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\setminus\{\neg\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
;

•

Since all the structures in
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{N}
are
(0,\emptyset)
-proper structures (and therefore, on those structures,
p
and
\neg\bar{p}
are equivalent),
\phi^{\prime}
also accepts
\mathcal{P}
and rejects
\mathcal{N}
.

In addition, all the turn-based structures in
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{N}
are self-looping, hence by Lemma
96
, we may assume that neither of the operators
\emptyset\lF
or
1,2\lG
occur in
\phi^{\prime}
. Furthermore, since
\phi^{\prime}
accepts the games
T_{p}\in\mathcal{P}
and
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\emptyset\lG,2\lG}\in\mathcal{P}
, by Lemma
104
, we have
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi^{\prime})=\{p\}
and
\phi^{\prime}
does not use the operators
\emptyset\lG,2\lG
. Furthermore, since
\phi^{\prime}
rejects the game structure
T_{2l+1:1,2}\in\mathcal{N}
, it does not use the operator
1,2\lF
(since all sub-formulas of
\phi^{\prime}
accept the state
q^{\mathsf{win}}
). In fact, the formula
\phi^{\prime}
is promising.

Since
\phi^{\prime}
accepts
T_{2l:1,2}\in\mathcal{P}
, and all the winning paths from the state
q_{2l}^{1,2}
are
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating, the formula
\phi^{\prime}
is
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating by Lemma
99
(Item a). Furthermore, we have
|\phi^{\prime}|\leq 3l+1-k
. Hence, since
\phi^{\prime}
rejects
T_{\mathsf{no}\;1\lG\geq k+1}\in\mathcal{N}
, by Lemma
108
,
\phi^{\prime}
is necessarily
(l,k)
-concise. We let
H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
be such that
|H|=k
and
\phi^{\prime}=\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(l,H)
. Then, consider some
1\leq i\leq n
. Since
\phi^{\prime}
accepts
T_{(l,C_{i},2)}\in\mathcal{P}
, it follows, by Lemma
110
, that
H\cap C_{i}\neq\emptyset
. This holds for all
1\leq i\leq n
. Therefore,
H
is a hitting set and
(l,C,k)
is a positive instance of the hitting set problem
\mathsf{Hit}
.
∎

Theorem
101
follows.

Proof.

This is direct consequence of Lemma
112
, of the fact that the instance
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(\lX)}_{(l,C,k)}
can be computed in logarithmic space from
(l,C,k)
, and of Theorem
65
.
∎

We believe that the result of Theorem
101
would also hold with
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}=\{\lF,\neg\}
or
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}=\{\lG,\neg\}
, but handling these cases would induce many additional technical difficulties. In particular, Theorem
65
can be not applied as is.

4.4.3
\mathsf{ATL}
learning with two agents and with one of the two operators
\lF
and
\lG

Let us now focus to the case of
\mathsf{ATL}
learning with two agents where only one of the two operators
\lF
ot
\lG
is allowed, without any negations. The goal of this subsection is to show the theorem below.

Theorem 113
.

For all
\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Bin}}^{\mathsf{lg}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, both decision problems
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lF\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
and
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lG\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
are
\mathsf{P}
-complete.

The idea is that with only the operator
\lF
(it is the same with only the operator
\lG
), given a bound
B\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
and a set of propositions
\mathsf{Prop}
, it is sufficient to consider only polynomially many
\mathsf{ATL}
-formulas. The reason why comes from Lemma
80
(that we have stated when considering
\mathsf{CTL}
-formulas). Indeed, this lemma states that, for all
\mathsf{ATL}
-formulas
\phi
, for two coalitions of agents
A_{1},A_{2}
, as soon as
A_{1}\subseteq A_{2}
or
A_{2}\subseteq A_{1}
, then
\langle\langle A_{1}\rangle\rangle\lF\langle\langle A_{2}\rangle\rangle\lF\phi\equiv\langle\langle A\rangle\rangle\lF\phi
, with
A\in\{A_{1},A_{2}\}
the largest of the two coalitions. However, since there are only two agents, there are only four coalitions:
\emptyset,\{1\},\{2\},\{1,2\}
. Furthermore, we can make the following observations, for formulas without binary operators:

•

If the operator
1,2\lF
is used, then all other
\lF
-operators are useless;

•

If at least one
\lF
-operator is used, using additional
\emptyset\lF
is useless;

•

Using twice in a row the operator
1\lF
or the operator
2\lF
is useless.

This implies that it is sufficient to consider only polynomially many
\mathsf{ATL}
-formulas. We define below the set of
\mathsf{ATL}
-formulas to consider.

Definition 114
.

Consider a set of propositions
\mathsf{Prop}
. We define the sets
\mathsf{ATL}_{\lF}(\mathsf{Prop},2)
and
\mathsf{ATL}_{\lG}(\mathsf{Prop},2)
below. For
\mathsf{H}\in\{\lF,\lG\}
, we define:

\mathsf{Quant}_{\mathsf{Alt}}^{\lF}:=\{\epsilon,\emptyset\lF,1,2\lF,(1\lF\cdot 2\lF)^{*},(1\lF\cdot 2\lF)^{*}\cdot 1\lF,(2\lF\cdot 1\lF)^{*},(2\lF\cdot 1\lF)^{*}\cdot 2\lF\}

and

\mathsf{Quant}_{\mathsf{Alt}}^{\lG}:=\{\epsilon,1,2\lG,\emptyset\lG,(1\lG\cdot 2\lG)^{*},(1\lG\cdot 2\lG)^{*}\cdot 1\lG,(2\lG\cdot 1\lG)^{*},(2\lG\cdot 1\lG)^{*}\cdot 2\lG\}

This definition satisfies the lemma below.

Lemma 115
.

Consider any set of propositions
\mathsf{Prop}
,
\mathsf{H}\in\{\lF,\lG\}
. For all sequences of quantifiers
\mathsf{Qt}\in\mathsf{Op}(\{\mathsf{H}\},2)^{*}
, there is some
\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Quant}_{\mathsf{Alt}}^{\mathsf{H}}
such that:

•

|\mathsf{Qt}|\leq|\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}|
;

•

for all
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}
-formulas
\phi
,
\mathsf{Qt}\cdot\phi\equiv\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}\cdot\phi
.

Proof.

We prove the result for
\mathsf{H}=\lF
, the case
\mathsf{H}=\lG
is analogous, and also relies entirely on Lemma
80
. There are several cases:

•

If
\mathsf{Qt}=\epsilon
, then
\mathsf{Qt}\in\mathsf{Quant}_{\mathsf{Alt}}^{\mathsf{H}}
.

•

Otherwise, assume that
\mathsf{Qt}
only features the operator
\emptyset\lF
. In that case, for all
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}
-formulas
\phi
, we have
\mathsf{Qt}\cdot\phi\equiv\emptyset\lF\cdot\phi
.

•

Otherwise, assume that
\mathsf{Qt}
features the operator
1,2\lF
. In that case, for all
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}
-formulas
\phi
, we have
\mathsf{Qt}\cdot\phi\equiv 1,2\lF\cdot\phi
.

•

Otherwise,
\mathsf{Qt}
does not feature the operator
1,2\lF
and features the operator
1\lF
or the operator
2\lF
. In that case, consider the sequence
\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}
obtained from
\mathsf{Qt}
by:

–

removing all
\emptyset\lF
operators;

–

shrinking all sequences in
(1\lF)^{+}
into
1\lF
and shrinking all sequences in
(2\lF)^{+}
into
2\lF
.

By Lemma
80
, for all
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}
-formulas
\phi
, the formula
\mathsf{Qt}\cdot\phi
is equivalent to
\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}\cdot\phi
, and
|\mathsf{Qt}|\leq|\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}|
.

∎

We handle in the definition below the case of formulas that may use a bounded amount of binary operators.

Definition 116
.

Consider some
\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Bin}}^{\mathsf{lg}}
and some
\mathsf{H}\in\{\lF,\lG\}
. We define inductively on
n\in\mathbb{N}
the set
\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},n)
as follows:

•

\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},0):=\{\mathsf{Qt}\cdot?\mid\mathsf{Qt}\in\mathsf{Quant}_{\mathsf{Alt}}^{\mathsf{H}}\}
;

•

For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},n+1):=\{\mathsf{Qt}\cdot(\gamma_{1}\bullet\gamma_{2})\mid\mathsf{Qt}\in\mathsf{Quant}_{\mathsf{Alt}}^{\mathsf{H}},\;\bullet\in\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\;\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},n)\}
.

For all sets of propositions
\mathsf{Prop}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\gamma\in\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},n)
, we say that an
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}
-formula
\phi
is
\mathsf{Prop}
-from

\gamma
if it is equal to
\gamma
, up to replacing every
?
with some proposition in
\mathsf{Prop}
.

For all bounds
B\in\mathbb{N}
, we let
\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},n,B)
denote the set of elements in
\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},n)
from which we can obtain a formula of size at most
B
.

This definition satisfies the lemma below.

Lemma 117
.

Consider some
\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Bin}}^{\mathsf{lg}}
and some
\mathsf{H}\in\{\lF,\lG\}
. For all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a polynom
q_{n}
such that, for all
B\in\mathbb{N}
,
|\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},n,B)|\leq q_{n}(n)
.

Furthermore, for all non-empty sets of propositions
\mathsf{Prop}
, for all
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}
-formulas
\phi
of size at most
B
, there is some
\gamma\in\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},n,B)
and a formula
\phi^{\prime}
of size at most
B
that is
\mathsf{Prop}
-obtained from
\gamma
and such that
\phi\equiv\phi^{\prime}
.

Proof.

The first part of the lemma is a direct consequence of the fact that the number of sequences in
\mathsf{Quant}_{\mathsf{Alt}}^{\mathsf{H}}
of size at most
B\in\mathbb{N}
is bounded by a polynomial in
B
.

The second part of the lemma is a direct consequence of Lemma
115
.
∎

We deduce that the learning problem for ATL with two agents and only one of the operators
\lF,\lG
can be decided in polynomial time.

Lemma 118
.

For all
\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Bin}}^{\mathsf{lg}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, both decision problems
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lF\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
and
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lG\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
can be decided in polynomial time.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{H}\in\{\lF,\lG\}
,
\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Bin}}^{\mathsf{lg}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Given an instance
\mathsf{In}=(\mathsf{Prop},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{N},B)
of the decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\mathsf{H}\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
, one can follow the following steps to check if
\mathsf{In}
is a positive instance:

1.

Loop over all elements
\gamma\in\mathsf{Rel}^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},\mathsf{H},n,B)
;

2.

Loop over all formulas
\phi
that can be
\mathsf{Prop}
-obtained from
\gamma
of size at most
B
;

3.

Check whether or not this formula
\phi
accepts
\mathcal{P}
and rejects
\mathcal{N}
.

Following these steps is enough to decide if
\mathsf{In}
is a positive instance of
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\mathsf{H}\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
by Lemma
117
. Furthermore, they can be executed in polynomial time. Indeed, by Lemma
117
, the loop of step 1) is entered polynomially many times. Furthermore, all formulas with at most
n
occurrences of binary operators use at most
2^{n}
propositions. Hence, the loop of step 2) is entered at most
|\mathsf{Prop}|^{2^{n}}
times, which is polynomial in
\mathsf{Prop}
since
n
is fixed. Finally, the last step can be done in polynomial time as well. Thus, we do obtain a polynomial-time procedure deciding the problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\mathsf{H}\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
.
∎

\mathsf{P}
-hardness
As mentioned for the
\mathsf{NL}
-hardness proof of the
\mathsf{CTL}
learning problem without
\lX
, to establish the
\mathsf{P}
-hardness, we are going to exhibit a reduction from the reachability problem in two-player games
\mathsf{Reach}
, introduced in Definition
91
. We define the reduction that we consider. Note the three turn-based structures that we define below are depicted in Figure
12
.

Definition 119
.

Consider two propositions
p,\bar{p}
and a proper
\{1,2\}
-turn-based structure
T
on
\{p,\bar{p}\}
. We let:

•

T_{\bar{p}}
be a trivial structure whose only state is labeled by the proposition
\bar{p}
;

•

T_{\mathsf{no}\lG}
be a two-state
(0,\emptyset)
-proper
\{1,2\}
-turn-based structure whose only starting state, labeled by
\bar{p}
, belongs to Agent 1, with two outgoing edges, one that loops, and one that goes to a self-looping sink labeled by
p
;

•

T_{\mathsf{no}\;\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle}
be a two-state
(0,\emptyset)
-proper
\{1,2\}
-turn-based structure similar to
T_{\mathsf{no}\lG}
, except that the starting state belongs to Agent 2, instead of Agent 1.

•

\mathcal{P}:=\{T,T_{\mathsf{no}\lG}\}
and
\mathcal{N}:=\{T_{\bar{p}},T_{\mathsf{no}\;\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle}\}
.

Then, we define the inputs
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(2),\lF}_{(p,\bar{p},T)}:=(\{p,\bar{p}\},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{N},2)
and
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(2),\lG}_{(p,\bar{p},T)}:=(\{p,\bar{p}\},\mathcal{N},\mathcal{P},2)
.

Figure 12:
The game
T_{\bar{p}}
on the left, the game
T_{\mathsf{no}\lG}
in the middle and the game
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle}
on the right.

The definition above satisfies the lemma below.

Lemma 120
.

Consider some set of unary operators
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}\subseteq\{\lX,\lF,\lG,\neg\}
, some set of binary operators
\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Bin}}^{\mathsf{lg}}
, some
n\in\mathbb{N}
and an input
(p,\bar{p},T)
of the decision problem
\mathsf{Reach}
. Let
\mathsf{H}\in\{\lF,\lG\}
. If
\mathsf{H}\in\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}
, the input
(p,\bar{p},T)
is a positive instance of the
\mathsf{Reach}
decision problem if and only if
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(2),\mathsf{H}}_{(p,\bar{p},T)}
is a positive instance of the
\mathsf{ATL}_{\mathsf{Learn}}^{2}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
decision problem.

Proof.

First assume that
\mathsf{H}=\lF
. Assume that
(p,\bar{p},T)
is a positive instance of
\mathsf{Reach}
. We let
\varphi:=\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF p
. We have
|\varphi|=2
. By assumption, we have
T\models\varphi
. In addition,
T_{\mathsf{no}\lG}\models\varphi
,
T_{\bar{p}}\not\models\varphi
and
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle}\not\models\varphi
. Hence,
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(2),\mathsf{F}}_{(p,\bar{p},T)}
is a positive instance of the
\mathsf{ATL}_{\mathsf{Learn}}^{2}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
decision problem.

On the other hand, assume that
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}^{2}(2),\mathsf{F}}_{(p,\bar{p},T)}
is a positive instance of the
\mathsf{ATL}_{\mathsf{Learn}}^{2}(\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
decision problem. Consider
\phi
a separating formula of size at most 2 that accepts
\mathcal{P}
and rejects
\mathcal{N}
. Let us show that, on
(0,\emptyset)
-proper structures, we have
\phi\implies\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF p
. If
\phi
is a proposition, uses a negation or a binary operator, then it is equivalent to either
p,\bar{p},\mathsf{True},\mathsf{False}
on
(0,\emptyset)
-proper structures. Since
\phi
accepts
T_{\bar{p}}
and rejects some negative structures, it necessarily is equivalent to
p
, and therefore it implies
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF p
. Assume now that
\phi
uses an operator in
\{\lX,\lF,\lG\}
(in which we necessarily have
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi)=\{p\}
). Since
\phi
accepts the structure
T_{\mathsf{no}\lG}
, it does not use the operator
\lG
. Therefore, if it does use an operator, it is not an
\lG
-operator, and therefore the coalition of agents used cannot contain Agent 2 since
\varphi
rejects the structure
T_{\mathsf{no}\;\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle}
. Therefore, we have
\varphi\in\{\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lX p,\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF p,\langle\langle\emptyset\rangle\rangle\lX p,\langle\langle\emptyset\rangle\rangle\lF p\}
. One can then check that, in all these cases, we have
\varphi\implies\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF p
. Therefore, since
T\models\varphi
, we also have
T\models\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF p
. Hence,
(p,\bar{p},T)
is a positive instance of
\mathsf{Reach}
.

The case
\mathsf{H}=\lG
is dual: in that case, we consider the formula
\varphi:=\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lG\bar{p}
(recall Proposition
59
).
∎

The proof of Theorem
113
is now direct.

Proof.

Let
\mathsf{H}\in\{\lF,\lG\}
. The decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\mathsf{H}\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
can be decided in polynomial time by Lemma
118
.

Furthermore, the decision problem
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\mathsf{H}\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
is
\mathsf{P}
-hard by Lemma
120
and the fact that the reduction given in Definition
119
can be computed in logarithmic space.
∎

4.4.4
\mathsf{ATL}
learning with three agents

Let us now consider the case of
\mathsf{ATL}
learning with three agents. The goal of this subsection is to show the lemma below.

Theorem 121
.

For all sets
\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}}\subseteq\mathsf{Op}_{\mathsf{Bin}}^{\mathsf{lg}}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, both decision problems
\mathsf{ATL}^{3}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lF\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
and
\mathsf{ATL}^{3}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lG\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},n)
are
\mathsf{NP}
-complete.

Overview of the reduction.

We present the reduction with the operator
\lF
. The reduction with the operator
\lG
is the same, up to reversing the sets of positive and negative structures. First, as for the
\mathsf{CTL}
and
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}
reductions, we follow the steps described in Section
4.2.1
, with Step
a)
already taken care of in Section
4.2.2
. Thus, we focus on
\mathsf{ATL}^{3}
-formulas using only the operator
\lF
(and a single proposition). First, we define turn-based structures ensuring that: the proposition used is
p
(with a trivial positive structure), and the operators
\langle\langle A\rangle\rangle\lF
such that
1\in A
and
\{2,3\}\cap A\neq\emptyset
are not used (with two negative structures). Furthermore, since all the structures that we use are self-looping, Lemma
96
gives that the operator
\langle\langle\emptyset\rangle\rangle\lF
is useless. Hence, we can focus on formulas using only the operators
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF,\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lF,\langle\langle\{3\}\rangle\rangle\lF,\langle\langle\{2,3\}\rangle\rangle\lF
and the proposition
p
, which are
\mathsf{Op}_{\{1\},\{2,3\}}(\{\lF\})
-formulas.

Fix an instance
(l,C,k)
of the hitting set problem. We consider the bound
B:=2l+1
. Our idea is to focus on
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating formulas. As for the
\mathsf{ATL}^{2}
case, we consider
T^{2l:\;1,2}
as positive structure and use Lemma
99
(Item a). Then, these
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating formulas feature at least
l
occurrences of the operator
\langle\langle\{1\}\rangle\rangle\lF
and
l
occurrences of operators
\langle\langle A\rangle\rangle\lF
with
2\in A
. Therefore the operator
\langle\langle\{3\}\rangle\rangle\lF
is not used.

Overall, the only thing that differs between the formulas that we consider is when the operators are
\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lF
and when the operators are
\langle\langle\{2,3\}\rangle\rangle\lF
. Thus, the formulas
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(l,H)
that we consider are such that the set
H
entirely determines at which index
i
an operator
\langle\langle\{2,3\}\rangle\rangle\lF
occurs (when
i\in H
), and at which index
i
an operator
\langle\langle\{2\}\rangle\rangle\lF
occurs (when
i\notin H
). To ensure that
|H|\leq k
, we consider
T^{2(k+1):\;1,3}
as negative structure and we use Lemma
99
(Item b).

Then, there remains to define, given a subset
C\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
, a positive turn-based structure
T_{l,C,3}
such that
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(l,H)
accepts
T_{l,C,3}
if and only if
H\cap C\neq\emptyset
. The structure
T_{l,C,3}
(see Figure
13
) is similar to the structure
T_{l,C,2}
, except that the testing states are Agent-3 states.

Formal definitions and proofs.
With the alternating turn-based structures that we have already defined (recall Definition
100
) it is actually sufficient for the reduction define one additional type of turn-based structures to encode the fact that hitting set intersect all sets. Before we define it, let us first define the shape of the
\mathsf{ATL}
-formulas that we will consider.

Definition 122
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}
. For all
H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
, an
\mathsf{ATL}
-formula is
an
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(l,H)
-formula if:

\phi=1\lF\;\langle\langle A_{l}\rangle\rangle\lF\cdots 1\lF\;\langle\langle A_{1}\rangle\rangle\lF p

where for all
i\in[1,\ldots,l]
, we have
A_{i}\in\{\{2\}.\{2,3\}\}
and
A_{i}=\{2,3\}
if and only if
i\in H
.

Let us now define the turn-based structure of interest whose definition is illustrated in Figure
13
. This definition, and the subsequent lemma and proof are very similar to what we did with the turn-based structure
T_{l,C,2}
from Definition
109
.

Definition 123
.

Let
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
and
C\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
. We let
T_{l,C,3}:=\langle Q^{l,C,3},I_{l,3},2,\{p\},\pi,\mathsf{AgSt},\mathsf{Succ}\rangle
where (recall that the states
q^{1,2}_{h}
come from the turn-based structure
T_{2l:\;1,2}
from Definition
100
):

•

Q^{l,C,3}:=\{q_{i}\mid 1\leq i\leq 2l\}\cup\{q^{1,2}_{h}\mid 1\leq h\leq 2l\}\cup\{q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1}\mid i\in C\}\cup\{q^{\mathsf{lose}},q^{\mathsf{win}}\}
;

•

I_{l}:=\{q_{2l}\}
;

•

For all
2\leq i\leq l+1
,
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2i}):=1
and
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2i-1}):=2
. For all
i\in C
, we have
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1}):=3
.

•

For all
1\leq i\leq l
, we have:

\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2i}):=\begin{cases}\{q_{2i},q_{2i-1}\}&\text{ if }i\notin C\\
\{q_{2i},q_{2i-1},q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1}\}&\text{ if }i\in C\\
\end{cases}

and

\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2i-1}):=\begin{cases}\{q_{2i-1},q_{2(i-1)}\}&\text{ if }i>1\\
\{q_{2i-1},q^{\mathsf{lose}}\}&\text{ if }i=1\\
\end{cases}

and, for all
i\in C
:

\mathsf{Succ}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1}):=\begin{cases}\{q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1},q^{1,2}_{2(i-1)}\}&\text{ if }i>1\\
\{q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i-1},q^{\mathsf{win}}\}&\text{ if }i=1\\
\end{cases}

•

For all
q\in Q^{l,C,3}\setminus\{q^{\mathsf{win}}\}
, we have
\pi(q):=\{\bar{p}\}
.

Figure 13:
The turn-based game structure
T_{3,\{1,2\},3}
.

The above definition satisfies the lemma below.

Lemma 124
.

Consider any
l\in\mathbb{N}_{1}
and
C,H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
and an
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(l,H)
-formula
\phi
. We have:

T_{l,C,3}\models\phi\text{ if and only if }H\cap C\neq\emptyset

Proof.

For all
1\leq i\leq l
, we let
H_{i}:=H\cap[1,\ldots,l]
. Then, in the turn-based structure
T_{l,C,3}
, we prove by induction on
1\leq i\leq l
the property
\mathcal{P}(i)
:
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(i,H_{i})
if and only if
H_{i}\cap C\neq\emptyset
. We first handle the case
i=1
. Consider the
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(1,H_{1})
-formula
\phi=1\lF\langle\langle A_{1}\rangle\rangle\lF p
. There are two cases.

•

Assume that
H_{1}\cap C=\{1\}
. Then, we have
A_{1}=\{2,3\}
. Furthermore,
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1}\in Q^{l,C,3}
with

q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1}\models 2,3\lF p
since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1})=3
and
q^{\mathsf{win}}\in\mathsf{Succ}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1})
. Thus,
q_{2}\models\phi
since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2})=1
.

•

Assume now that
H_{1}\cap C=\emptyset
. If
1\in C
, we have
1\notin H
, thus
A_{1}=\{2\}
. Since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1})=3
and
\pi(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1})=\{\bar{p}\}
, we have
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{1}\not\models\langle\langle A_{1}\rangle\rangle\lF p
. Hence,
q_{2}\not\models\phi
. On the other hand, if
1\notin C
, we have
\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2})=\{q_{2},q_{1}\}
and
\mathsf{Succ}(q_{1})=\{q_{1},q^{\mathsf{lose}}\}
. Therefore,
q_{2}\not\models\phi
.

Hence, the property
\mathcal{P}(1)
holds. Assume now that
\mathcal{P}(i)
holds for some
1\leq i\leq l-1
. Consider the
\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(i+1,H_{i+1})
-formula
\phi
defined by:

\phi:=1\lF\;\langle\langle A_{i+1}\rangle\rangle\lF\phi^{\prime}

with
A_{i+1}=\{2,3\}
if
i+1\in H
and
A_{i+1}=\{2\}
otherwise, and
\phi^{\prime}=\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(i,H_{i})
. As above, there are two cases.

•

Assume that
i+1\in H\cap C
. Since there is a safe winning path from
q_{2i}^{1,2}
that is
(\{1\},\{2\},2i)
-alternating and the formula
\phi^{\prime}
is
(\{1\},\{2\},2i)
-alternating, it follows that
q_{2i}^{1,2}\models\phi^{\prime}
, by Lemma
99
(Item b). Hence, we have
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i+1}\in Q^{l,C,3}
with
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i+1}\models 2,3\lF\phi^{\prime}
. Therefore,
q_{2(i+1)}\models 1\lF\langle\langle A_{i+1}\rangle\rangle\lF\phi^{\prime}
since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2(i+1)})=1
.

•

Assume now that
i+1\notin H\cap C
. Let us show that
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi
if and only if
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\prime}
. First, if
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\prime}
, then
q_{2i+1}\models\langle\langle A_{i+1}\rangle\rangle\lF\phi^{\prime}
since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q_{2i+1})=2\in A_{i+1}
. Thus, we have
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi
. Assume now that
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi
. Note that, the winning paths from
q_{2(i+1)}
are all
(\{1\},\{2,3\},2(i+1))
-alternating, therefore, by Lemma
99
(Item a), no strict sub-formula of
\phi
accept the state
q_{2(i+1)}
. Similarly, the winning paths from
q_{2i+1}
are all
(\{2,3\},\{1\},2i+1)
-alternating, therefore, by Lemma
99
(Item a), no strict sub-formula of
\langle\langle A_{i+1}\rangle\rangle\lF\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(2)}(i,H_{i})
accept the state
q_{2i+1}
. Then, there are two cases.

–

If
i+1\in C
, we have
i+1\notin H
, thus
\phi=1\lF 2\lF\phi^{\prime}
. Furthermore, the winning paths from
q_{2i}^{1,2}
are all
(\{1\},\{2,3\},2(i+1))
-alternating, therefore, by Lemma
99
(Item a), no strict sub-formula of
\phi^{\prime}
accept the state
q_{2i}^{1,2}
. Thus, since
\mathsf{AgSt}(q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i+1})=3
, it follows that
q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i+1}\not\models 2\lF\phi^{\prime}
. Since
\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2(i+1)})=\{q_{2(i+1)},q_{2i+1},q^{\mathsf{Test}}_{2i+1}\}
and
\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2i+1})=\{q_{2i+1},q_{2i}\}
, we have
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\prime}
.

–

If
i+1\notin C
, then
\mathsf{Succ}(q_{2(i+1)})=\{q_{2(i+1)},q_{2i+1}\}
, we have that
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\prime}
.

We have established that
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi
if and only if
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\prime}
, with
\phi^{\prime}=\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(i,H_{i})
. Furthermore, by
\mathcal{P}(i)
, we have
q_{2i}\models\phi^{\prime}
if and only if
H_{i}\cap C\neq\emptyset
. Since
i+1\notin H\cap C
, it follows that
H_{i+1}\cap C=H_{i}\cap C
. Hence, we do obtain that
q_{2(i+1)}\models\phi
if and only if
H_{i+1}\cap C\neq\emptyset
.

Hence,
\mathcal{P}(i+1)
holds. In fact,
\mathcal{P}(i)
holds for all
1\leq i\leq l
. The lemma follows.
∎

Definition of the reduction

We can now define the reductions that we consider for the two cases
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}=\{\lF\}
and
\mathsf{U}^{\mathsf{t}}=\{\lG\}
.

Definition 125
.

Consider an instance
(l,C,k)
of the hitting set problem
\mathsf{Hit}
. We define:

•

\mathcal{P}:=\{T_{p},T_{2l:1,2}\}\cup\{T_{(l,C_{i},3)}\mid 1\leq i\leq n\}
;

•

\mathcal{N}:=\{T_{2(l+1):1,2},T_{2(k+1):1,3}\}
.

Then, we define the inputs
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(3),\lF}_{(l,C,k)}:=(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\mathcal{P},\mathcal{N},2l+3)
and
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(3),\lG}_{(l,C,k)}:=(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\mathcal{N},\mathcal{P},2l+3)
.

This definition satisfies the lemma below.

Lemma 126
.

Let
\mathsf{H}\in\{\lF,\lG\}
. An instance
(l,C,k)
of the hitting set problem is positive if and only if
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(3),\mathsf{H}}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of the
\mathsf{ATL}^{3}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\mathsf{H}\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
decision problem if and only if
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(3),\mathsf{H}}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of the
\mathsf{ATL}^{3}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
decision problem.

Proof.

We consider the case where
\mathsf{H}=\lF
, the case
\mathsf{H}=\lG
is analogous.

Assume that
(l,C,k)
is a positive instance of the hitting set problem
\mathsf{Hit}
. Consider a hitting set
H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
and the
\phi:=\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3),\lF}(l,H)\in\mathsf{ATL}^{3}(\{p\},\{\lF\},\emptyset,\mathsf{B}^{\mathsf{l}},0)
. We have
|\phi|=2l+1
. Furthermore:

•

the proposition used in
\phi
is
p
, therefore
\phi
accepts the structure
T_{p}
;

•

\phi
is
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating, thus it accepts the structure
T_{2l:1,2}
by Lemma
99
(Item b);

•

for
1\leq i\leq n
, we have
C_{i}\cap H\neq\emptyset
, hence by Lemma
124
,
\phi
accepts the structure
T_{(l,C_{i},3)}
;

•

|H|\leq k
, hence there are at most
k
times an operator
\langle\langle A\rangle\rangle\lF
used in
\phi
with
3\in A
. Hence,
\phi
is not
(\{1\},\{3\},2(k+1))
-alternating. Thus, it rejects the structure
T_{2(k+1):1,3}
by Lemma
99
(Item a).

Therefore,
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(3),\lF}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of the
\mathsf{ATL}^{3}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lF\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
decision problem.

Straightforwardly, if
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(3),\lF}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of
\mathsf{ATL}^{3}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lF\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
, then it is also a positive instance of
\mathsf{ATL}^{3}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
.

Assume now that
\mathsf{In}^{\mathsf{ATL}(3),\lF}_{(l,C,k)}
is a positive instance of
\mathsf{ATL}^{3}_{\mathsf{Learn}}(\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
. Consider a formula
\phi\in\mathsf{ATL}^{3}(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
with
|\phi|\leq 2l+1
that accepts
\mathcal{P}
and rejects
\mathcal{N}
. We let
\phi=\mathsf{Qt}\cdot x
for some
\mathsf{Qt}\in(\mathsf{Op}(3,\{\lF,\lG\}))^{*}
and
x\in\mathsf{Prop}_{0}
. Let
(\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime},x^{\prime}):=\mathsf{UnNeg}(\mathsf{Qt},0)
. We let
y\in\mathsf{Prop}_{0}
be such that
y=x
if and only if
x^{\prime}=0
. Finally, we let
\phi^{\prime}:=\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}\cdot y
. By Lemma
61
, we have:

•

|\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}|\leq|\mathsf{Qt}|
, therefore
|\phi^{\prime}|\leq|\phi|\leq 2l+1
;

•

\mathsf{Qt}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Op}(3,\{\lF,\lG\})
, therefore
\phi^{\prime}\in\mathsf{ATL}^{3}(\mathsf{Prop}_{0},\{\lF,\lG\},\emptyset,\emptyset,0)
;

•

Since all the structures in
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{N}
are
(0,\emptyset)
-proper structures (and therefore, on those structures,
p
and
\neg\bar{p}
are equivalent),
\phi^{\prime}
also accepts
\mathcal{P}
and rejects
\mathcal{N}
.

The formula
\phi^{\prime}
accepts the structure
T_{p}
, therefore
\mathsf{Prop}(\phi^{\prime})=\{p\}
. Furthermore, the formula
\phi
rejects both structures
T_{2(l+1):1,2}
and
T_{2(k+1):1,3}
. Hence, for all operators
\langle\langle A\rangle\rangle\lF
used in
\phi
, if
1\in A
, then
\{2,3\}\cap A=\emptyset
. Therefore, since
\phi
accepts the structure
T_{2l:1,2}
, by Lemma
99
(Item a), the formula
\phi
is
(\{1\},\{2\},2l)
-alternating. Since we have
|\phi|\leq 2l+1
, this implies that

\phi=\langle\langle A^{1}_{1}\rangle\rangle\lF\langle\langle A^{2}_{1}\rangle\rangle\lF\ldots\langle\langle A^{1}_{l}\rangle\rangle\lF\langle\langle A^{2}_{l}\rangle\rangle\lF p

where, for all
1\leq i\leq l
, we have
A^{1}_{i}=\{1\}
and
2\in A^{2}_{i},1\notin A^{2}_{i}
. However, since
\phi
rejects the structure
T_{2(k+1):1,3}
, by Lemma
99
(Item b), we have that
\phi
is not
(\{1\},\{3\},2(k+1))
-alternating. Hence, there are at most
k
indices
1\leq i\leq l
such that
3\in A^{2}_{i}
. This implies that
\phi=\phi^{\mathsf{ATL}(3)}(l,H)
for a set
H\subseteq[1,\ldots,l]
such that
|H|\leq k
. Consider then any
1\leq i\leq n
. Since the formula
\phi
accepts the structure
T_{(l,C_{i},3)}
, it follows by Lemma
124
that
C_{i}\cap H\neq\emptyset
. In fact,
H
is a hitting set and
(l,C,k)
is a positive instance of the hitting set problem
\mathsf{Hit}
.

The case
\mathsf{H}=\lG
is analogous. (It suffices to consider the negation of the separating formula.)
∎

The proof of Theorem
121
is now direct.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemmas
126
, the fact that the reductions from Definition
125
can be computed in logarithmic space and Theorem
65
.
∎

5
Conclusion and future Work

In this work, we undertake an in-depth complexity analysis of the passive learning problems for
\mathsf{LTL}
,
\mathsf{CTL}
and
\mathsf{ATL}
. Our results are gathered in Table
1
, and could be roughly summarized as follows. When the number of occurrences of binary operators is unbounded, all the learning problems are
\mathsf{NP}
-complete. On the other hand, when the number of occurrences of binary operators is bounded, discrepancies between the behaviors of
\mathsf{LTL}
,
\mathsf{CTL}
, and
\mathsf{ATL}
learning appear: there are subsets of operators for which the learning problem is tractable with some number of agents, while it becomes untractable with more agents.

Overall, this paper essentially tackles reductions and hardness proofs, while the arguments that specific problems are in
\mathsf{L},\mathsf{NL},\mathsf{P}
are (relatively) more straightforward. However, this is made possible by the fact that the bound in the size of the formula is given in unary. We have argued in Section
3.1.1
why we believe that it makes sense to consider such a setting. Nonetheless, the decision problems that would arise with a bound given in binary would certainly be interesting and challenging research questions (just like it was in
[
24
]
). Another interesting direction, which can be combined with the above one, could be, as is done in
[
24
]
, to study the existence of tractable approximation algorithms.
